# 13.ServiceDefaults — State Map

> **What this file is:** Phase and task tracker for all work within `13.ServiceDefaults`.
> **What it is not:** The root tracker — that lives at `state-map.md`.
> **Sync policy:** When all tasks under a Phase Key are `●`, run `/state-map-phase` with `phase_key: SK.13.{Phase}` to propagate that milestone to the root state-map.

---

## Legend

| Symbol | Meaning |
|--------|---------|
| `○` | Not started |
| `◐` | In progress |
| `●` | Complete |
| `⚑` | Blocked |
| `—` | N/A / Skipped |

---

## Phase Key Registry

> Phase keys are the sync bridge between this sub-state-map and the root `state-map.md`.
> Each key maps a local milestone to a root-level phase. When a key's Promotion Condition is met, the root is updated via `/state-map-phase`.

| Phase Key | Maps to Root Phase | Promotion Condition |
|-----------|-------------------|-------------------|
| `SK.13.Design` | Design | All tasks in Phase: Design are `●` |
| `SK.13.Scaffold` | Scaffold | All tasks in Phase: Scaffold are `●` |
| `SK.13.Core` | Core | All tasks in Phase: Core are `●` |
| `SK.13.Tests` | Tests | All tasks in Phase: Tests are `●` |
| `SK.13.Docs` | Docs | All tasks in Phase: Docs are `●` |
| `SK.13.Published` | Published | All tasks in Phase: Published are `●` |
| `SK.13.WO084` | P-531–P-537 (WO-084) | All tasks in Phase: WO-084 are `●` |
| `SK.13.WO086` | P-565, P-566, P-569, P-574, P-575 (WO-086) | All tasks in Phase: WO-086 are `●` |

GENUINE FINDING: RequestLocalizationOptions lives in namespace Microsoft.AspNetCore.Builder, not Microsoft.AspNetCore.Localization (an ASP.NET Core namespace-placement gotcha, same class as the AddRateLimiter/Microsoft.AspNetCore.Builder precedent already recorded in this agent's own memory) — required an explicit `using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Builder;`. Two paths: (1) direct Kestrel TLS termination via `AddMtlsClientCertificate(mode)`, wiring `KestrelServerOptions.ConfigureHttpsDefaults` and delegating accept/reject to `IMtlsCertificateValidator`; (2) ingress-forwarded-header termination via `AddMtlsForwardedHeaderCertificate`/`MtlsForwardedHeaderMiddleware`, reading a **consumer-configured, never vendor-defaulted** header name. **Genuinely blocked, not merely design-ahead-of-implementation like most of this domain's prior cross-domain phases:** `SharedKernel.Security.Mtls` does not exist on disk at all — no placeholder `.csproj`, no `.slnx` registration — a stronger blocker than the `08.Storage`/`09.Search`/`10.Intelligence` precedents. Two halves: (1) gRPC — `WithTracing(t => t.AddGrpcClientInstrumentation())`, activating `OpenTelemetry.Instrumentation.GrpcNetClient` (already referenced by `11.Communication.Grpc.csproj` at `1.15.1-beta.1` but never invoked anywhere — a confirmed dead-config-knob-shaped gap found during `arch-lead`'s WO-056 review) to enrich outbound gRPC spans with RPC semantic-convention tags instead of the under-specified generic-HTTP span they get today; requires a NEW `PackageReference` to that same package on `SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults.csproj` itself (S-18) — the family's first member needing a real package, not just a string. (2) Polly — `WithTracing(t => t.AddSource("Polly"))` + `WithMetrics(m => m.AddMeter("Polly"))`, activating Polly v8's own resilience telemetry (retry/circuit-breaker/timeout events) already latent inside `SharedKernel.Communication.Rest`'s `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Resilience` dependency — pure string-name wiring, zero new package, mirroring `WithMessagingTelemetry`'s pre-existing third-party `"MassTransit"`-name precedent. **Zero cross-domain gate** — see Blocked section. **Flagged idempotency risk unique to this method:** `.AddGrpcClientInstrumentation()` is an instrumentation-factory registration, not a bare `AddSource(string)` call, so it does not automatically inherit the OTel SDK's by-name dedup that keeps every other `With*Telemetry` sibling idempotent for free — C-47 must empirically verify and, if needed, guard this. | D-37 | **VERIFICATION + AC#3 REFUTATION RECORD (P-503/WO-081).** Verified against real, shipped source (`Cryptography/KeyVaultKeyProviderExtensions.cs` and `01.Core`'s `AzureKeyVaultCryptographyServiceCollectionExtensions.cs`, not assumed): `AddSharedKernelKeyVaultKeyProvider` is a thin idempotent call-through to `01.Core`'s `AddSharedKernelAzureKeyVaultCryptography(IConfiguration)`, which registers **one** `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` singleton and wires it, RAW and UNCACHED, as all three of `IEncryptionKeyProvider`/`IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider`/`IEncryptionKeyProviderProbe` — `01.Core`'s own XML doc states this explicitly ("Registers no caching decorator... wrap explicitly if desired"). So P-503's AC#1/#2 premise is CONFIRMED TRUE: the gap is real. **AC#3 ("a service using this extension satisfies P-498's startup fail-fast check without extra manual wiring") is FALSE and must be corrected, not implemented as written.** Read directly against `06.Persistence/state-map.md`'s design-locked D-131 (P-498, same WO-081 wave, SEVERE-defect correction): the accidental ambient-registration-order collision between `.WithEncryption()` and this method — both registering the SAME unkeyed `IEncryptionKeyProvider` — is P-498's own named root cause, and its fix is a structural DI-isolation change making that ambient slot STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE from `.WithEncryption()` in either mode, replaced by an EXPLICIT, deliberate opt-in call the consuming service makes on its OWN `EfCorePersistenceBuilder` — `.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<TProvider>()`, resolving `TProvider` (a concrete type, e.g. `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`) from the ambient/unkeyed container by the CONSUMER's own explicit choice. There is no way for `AddSharedKernelKeyVaultKeyProvider` to make that connection "automatic" without recreating the exact ambient-collision hazard D-131 was written to eliminate — doing so would be actively wrong, not merely unnecessary. **Corrected scope for this phase:** the caching fix (D-38) must be COMPATIBLE with and never fight `06.Persistence`'s keyed-DI isolation — confirmed compatible, since D-131's own `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider` explicitly documents that its wrapped `TProvider` "may optionally already be `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`-wrapped — doesn't matter, only its async members are ever called." AC#3 is replaced with: document (never auto-wire) that a service wanting BOTH KMS-backed general-purpose crypto AND KMS-backed persistence-layer column encryption must call `.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<TProvider>()` itself, explicitly, in its own `06.Persistence` builder chain (D-39 covers the doc) | WO-081 (P-503) | SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults | `●` |
| D-38 | **Design the `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` default-wrap, closing the confirmed gap.** `AddSharedKernelKeyVaultKeyProvider` gains an optional `TimeSpan? cacheTtl = null` parameter. After calling through to `01.Core`'s `AddSharedKernelAzureKeyVaultCryptography` (unchanged), and unless the effective TTL is `TimeSpan.Zero` (explicit opt-out — falls back to the raw, unwrapped provider for `IEncryptionKeyProvider` too, mirroring `K8sServiceDiscoveryOptions.EndpointCacheTtlSeconds`'s existing "0 disables" convention from `11.Communication`), this method: (1) registers `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Symmetric.CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` as its OWN concrete singleton (`services.AddSingleton(sp => new CachedEncryptionKeyProvider(sp.GetRequiredService<AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider>(), sp.GetService<TimeProvider>() ?? TimeProvider.System, effectiveTtl))`) — needed so `06.Persistence`'s `.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<TProvider>()` can ALSO target the cached variant explicitly via `.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<CachedEncryptionKeyProvider>()`, alongside the still-independently-available raw `.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider>()`; (2) re-registers **only** `IEncryptionKeyProvider` to resolve the cached wrapper (`services.AddSingleton<IEncryptionKeyProvider>(sp => sp.GetRequiredService<CachedEncryptionKeyProvider>())` — the BCL container resolves the LAST registration for a single-instance request, so this cleanly supersedes `01.Core`'s own raw `IEncryptionKeyProvider` registration for THIS interface only, without touching `01.Core`'s own registration call). `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider` and `IEncryptionKeyProviderProbe` are DELIBERATELY LEFT resolving the RAW `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` singleton, untouched — two independent, load-bearing reasons: `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` (`01.Core`, P-446) implements `IEncryptionKeyProvider` ONLY, never `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider` (envelope wrap/unwrap is a real per-call crypto operation against the vault, not a cacheable key lookup, so there is nothing to wrap); and a readiness/health probe caching its own reachability signal would defeat the entire purpose of `AddKeyVaultKeyProviderReadinessCheck` — a probe must always observe live KMS state. `cacheTtl`: `null` → internal default (5 minutes, matching the TTL value already used throughout `01.Core`'s own `CachedEncryptionKeyProviderTests`); a negative value throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` (mirrors `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`'s own constructor guard) before any registration occurs. **Confirmed this can never unlock any synchronous path** — `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` never implements `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider` (`01.Core`'s P-492 marker) regardless of cache warmth or TTL, so wrapping here has zero effect on `01.Core`'s `EncryptionKeyProviderCapabilities.IsGenuinelySynchronous` gate — this fix is valuable strictly for ASYNC consumers (`ISymmetricEncryptionService.EncryptAsync`/`DecryptAsync` general-purpose callers, `06.Persistence`'s `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider.WarmCurrentAsync`/`WarmVersionAsync` if the consumer opts into the cached `TProvider`) | WO-081 (P-503) | SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults | `●` |
| D-39 | **Design the cross-domain KMS/hard-sync composition-root hazard warning — documentation-only, no code fix (out of this domain's jurisdiction to fix; the collision, if reachable, lives inside `07.Messaging`'s own DI resolution code, that domain's own P-499).** This wave's calibration note (verified by `07.Messaging` via reflection against the installed MassTransit assembly, cited in this phase's own dispatch input): `07.Messaging`'s payload-encryption serializer path is HARD-SYNCHRONOUS with no async overload, and therefore structurally incompatible with ANY KMS-backed `IEncryptionKeyProvider` after `01.Core`'s P-492 ships — a KMS-backed provider (cached or not, per D-38's own finding) can never satisfy `IsGenuinelySynchronous`, so `07.Messaging`'s `WithPayloadTransform()` throws `NotSupportedException` unconditionally, forever, the moment its resolved `IEncryptionKeyProvider`/`ISymmetricEncryptionService` is KMS-backed — not a performance hazard, a hard functional break identical in shape to the one D-126/D-131 (`06.Persistence`) found and fixed structurally. `13.ServiceDefaults` is the one place a service composes BOTH `AddSharedKernelKeyVaultKeyProvider()` (this method) and `07.Messaging`'s builder in the same `Program.cs`, and is therefore the right place to document — IN CAPITALS, mirroring this platform's established hazard-labeling convention — that enabling this method makes the ambient, unkeyed `IEncryptionKeyProvider`/`ISymmetricEncryptionService` KMS-backed and ASYNC-ONLY-SAFE, and that a service also enabling `07.Messaging` payload encryption against that SAME ambient slot will break unconditionally; the two must be kept on independently-configured `IEncryptionKeyProvider` instances (config-backed for messaging, KMS-backed for this method) until/unless `07.Messaging` ships its own keyed-DI isolation mirroring `06.Persistence`'s D-131. This task adds no functional code — XML doc + README + `CLAUDE.md` warning only (DO-32 covers the write-up) | WO-081 (P-503) | SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults | `●` |

## Phase: WO-084 — Per-Integration Package Split (BREAKING, package topology only) <!-- phase-key: SK.13.WO084 -->

> **Why:** a probe project referencing `SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults` and nothing else restored **25 SharedKernel projects and 73 NuGet packages**, 54 of them heavyweight (`MassTransit` ×6, `Azure.Messaging.ServiceBus`, `Microsoft.Identity.Web` ×8, EF Core, `Temporalio` ×4, `Quartz` ×3, `StackExchange.Redis`) — all from 14 unconditional `ProjectReference`s on the composition root. Two of them were dead: no source file used `SharedKernel.Messaging.MassTransit` or `SharedKernel.Primitives`.
> **Shape:** a base that references no SharedKernel package, plus thirteen integration packages each referencing the base and the one package it integrates. Namespaces are preserved, so a consumer adds a `PackageReference` and changes no source. Full rationale and the topology table are on root `state-map.md` P-531.
> Root phases: P-531 Design · P-532 Scaffold · P-533 Core · P-534 Tests · P-535 Governance (tracked in `00.Governance/state-map.md`) · P-536 Docs · P-537 Verification.

| ID | Task | Work Order | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-40 | Ratify the topology: base keeps `AddServiceDefaults`, every `WithXTelemetry` (all wire by string name, so moving them would shrink no closure), `BaggageLogRecordProcessor`, `StartupGate`, health endpoints, `HealthCheckNames`/`HealthCheckTags`, rate limiting. Thirteen integration packages named by one rule: `SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults.` + the integrated package's capability, plus its provider segment where that package is provider-specific | WO-084/P-531 | SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults (all) | `●` |
| D-41 | Namespaces preserved on every moved type (WO-082 Guards→Core precedent) — migration is a `PackageReference`, never a source edit | WO-084/P-531 | SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults (all) | `●` |
| D-42 | `HealthCheckRegistrationLogging` made public instead of `InternalsVisibleTo`: internals exposed across separately-published packages bind an integration to one exact base build, and NuGet's minimum-version resolution lets the two drift, surfacing as `MissingMethodException` at runtime | WO-084/P-531 | SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults | `●` |
| D-43 | EventIds preserved: 13000/13001/13003 move to `.Security.Mtls`, 13004 to `.Localization`, 13002 stays in the base. The ServiceDefaults family shares 13000–13099, allocated per EventId and never reused — forced, since fifteen packages cannot each take a 100-wide sub-block inside 13000–13999, which holds ten | WO-084/P-531 | SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults.Security.Mtls, .Localization | `●` |

> **2026-09-08 (WO-081, P-503):** `servicedefaults-arch-planner` processed the root Phase Backlog's `P-503` as part of the coordinated cross-domain breaking wave WO-081 (`01.Core`'s AAD/sync-gate/Key-Vault-hardening phases already design-locked this session; `06.Persistence`'s P-498 SEVERE-defect correction, D-131, in the same session). **The phase input's own AC#3 was found stale/false and refuted, not implemented as written** — see D-37's REFUTATION RECORD: `06.Persistence`'s corrected P-498 design deliberately makes the connection between this method's ambient `IEncryptionKeyProvider` and `.WithEncryption()`'s persistence-layer encryption an EXPLICIT, consumer-driven opt-in (`.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<TProvider>()`), never something this method can or should auto-satisfy — attempting to would recreate the exact ambient-collision hazard `06.Persistence`'s own fix eliminated. **The confirmed real gap (AC#1/#2) is closed:** `AddSharedKernelKeyVaultKeyProvider` gains an optional `cacheTtl` parameter (D-38) wrapping only the `IEncryptionKeyProvider` resolution in `01.Core`'s `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` by default (5-minute default, `TimeSpan.Zero` disables, negative throws) — `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider`/`IEncryptionKeyProviderProbe` deliberately stay on the raw provider (neither is implemented by `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`, and a probe must never be cache-masked); `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` is also registered as its own concrete type so `06.Persistence`'s explicit opt-in can target either the raw or the cached provider by its own choice — coordination, not collision, confirmed directly against `06.Persistence`'s own `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider` design note that a cache-wrapped `TProvider` is explicitly tolerated. **New cross-domain hazard surfaced and documented (D-39), not fixed here (out of jurisdiction):** this wave's `07.Messaging` calibration finding — its payload-encryption serializer path is hard-synchronous with no async overload — means a service enabling both this method and `07.Messaging` payload encryption against the same ambient `IEncryptionKeyProvider` breaks unconditionally (`NotSupportedException`, forever) once `01.Core`'s P-492 ships; documented IN CAPITALS at the one composition-root location a developer would compose both. **Zero cross-domain gate for the Core/Tests/Docs implementation itself** — no new `PackageReference`/`ProjectReference` needed (`CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` already transitively reachable). `SK.13.Design` +3 (D-37/D-38/D-39, all `●`-locked in the same pass, per this domain's own documented-ahead-of-implementation-when-genuinely-unblocked precedent); `SK.13.Core` +1 (C-70, `○`); `SK.13.Tests` +1 (T-82, `○`); `SK.13.Docs` +1 (DO-32, `○`); `SK.13.Scaffold`/`SK.13.Published` unaffected. **Not a breaking API change** (new parameter is optional, additive) but IS a default BEHAVIOR CHANGE for existing callers of `AddSharedKernelKeyVaultKeyProvider()` (their ambient `IEncryptionKeyProvider` becomes cache-wrapped by default where it was previously raw) — flag for release notes at the next `devops-lead` publish pass, mirroring the WO-054/P-351 precedent of documenting a behavior note without performing the repack in this planning session (servicedefaults-arch-planner, WO-081, P-503).
>
> **2026-09-08 (WO-081/P-503, resume — the final phase of WO-081 for this domain):** `SK.13.Core`/`SK.13.Tests`/`SK.13.Docs` all promoted `◐` → `●` — C-70/T-82/DO-32 implemented and tested in one session, zero deviation from D-37/D-38/D-39's already-locked contracts. `AddSharedKernelKeyVaultKeyProvider` gained the optional `cacheTtl` parameter exactly per D-38; the two pre-existing `KeyVaultKeyProviderExtensionsTests` cases asserting `IEncryptionKeyProvider`'s registration count as 1 were corrected to assert 2 (the raw registration from `01.Core`'s call-through, plus this method's own additive re-registration redirecting to the cached wrapper — MS.DI resolves the LAST registration for a single-instance request), with the reasoning recorded inline rather than silently changed. 7 new resolution-level tests added (T-82), each requiring a syntactically-valid-but-never-network-reached `AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions` configuration to get past `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`'s eager `IOptions<T>.Value` read. Full solution build (`Platform.SharedKernel.slnx`, 0 errors) and `SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults.Tests` (214/214 passing, 0 regressions) both verified via real `dotnet build`/`dotnet test` — no substitute harness needed. `SharedKernel.MultiTenancy` untouched. All six `SK.13.*` phase keys are now `●`/`—` again — **this closes WO-081/P-503 end to end for this domain.** Root-owned files (`state-map.md`, root `CLAUDE.md`, `Directory.Packages.props`, `Platform.SharedKernel.slnx`) were not touched per this wave's explicit protocol — root propagation (promoting `13.ServiceDefaults`'s Core/Tests/Docs rows, and closing root Phase Backlog `P-503` to `●` Complete) is left to the coordinator. ## Changelog

> One line per session. Format: `[YYYY-MM-DD] {what changed} — {trigger}`.

- [2026-09-26] WO-086 recorded (P-565, P-566, P-569, P-574, P-575): request context and correlation id owned by `.Security` (`UseSharedKernelRequestContext()`), `ITenantProvider` removed, per-dependency readiness packages deleted in favour of `IReadinessProbe` + `AddSharedKernelReadiness()`, every package Host tier, docs rewritten — WO-086 foundation refactor

- [2026-09-08] WO-081/P-503 processed (cross-domain breaking wave — `01.Core`'s AAD/sync-gate/Key-Vault-hardening phases and `06.Persistence`'s P-498 SEVERE-defect correction both design-locked earlier the same session): **AC#3 refuted, not implemented as written (D-37).** The phase input claimed the fix should make a service "satisfy `06.Persistence`'s P-498 startup fail-fast check without extra manual wiring" — false against `06.Persistence`'s own design-locked D-131, which deliberately replaces an ambient-registration-order collision (this method and `.WithEncryption()` both registering the SAME unkeyed `IEncryptionKeyProvider`) with an EXPLICIT, consumer-driven opt-in (`.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<TProvider>()`) precisely to eliminate that collision — making it automatic here would recreate the exact hazard `06.Persistence` just fixed. **Real gap (AC#1/#2) confirmed and closed (D-38):** direct source read of `Cryptography/KeyVaultKeyProviderExtensions.cs` and `01.Core`'s `AzureKeyVaultCryptographyServiceCollectionExtensions.cs` confirms `AddSharedKernelKeyVaultKeyProvider` wires `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` raw and uncached as `IEncryptionKeyProvider`/`IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider`/`IEncryptionKeyProviderProbe` — `01.Core`'s own doc says "wrap explicitly if desired," a deliberate gap this composition-root method is the right place to close. New optional `cacheTtl` parameter wraps ONLY `IEncryptionKeyProvider` in `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` (5-minute internal default, `TimeSpan.Zero` disables, negative throws); `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider`/`IEncryptionKeyProviderProbe` deliberately stay on the raw provider (neither is implemented by `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`, and a probe must never cache its own reachability signal); `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` also registered as its own concrete singleton so `06.Persistence`'s `.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<TProvider>()` can target either the raw or cached variant by its own explicit choice — confirmed compatible, never fighting, `06.Persistence`'s own D-131/D-129 design note that a `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`-wrapped `TProvider` is explicitly tolerated ("only its async members are ever called"), and confirmed this can never unlock any synchronous path (`CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` never implements `01.Core`'s P-492 `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider` marker regardless of cache warmth). **New cross-domain hazard surfaced, documented not fixed (D-39):** this wave's `07.Messaging` calibration finding (its payload-encryption serializer is hard-synchronous, no async overload, verified by reflection against the installed MassTransit assembly) means a service enabling both this method and `07.Messaging` payload encryption against the same ambient `IEncryptionKeyProvider` breaks unconditionally once `01.Core`'s P-492 ships — documented IN CAPITALS at the one composition-root location a developer would compose both; the actual fix, if any, is `07.Messaging`'s own P-499 territory. Zero cross-domain gate for implementation — no new `PackageReference`/`ProjectReference` needed. `SK.13.Design` +3 (D-37/D-38/D-39, all `●`); `SK.13.Core` +1 (C-70, `○`); `SK.13.Tests` +1 (T-82, `○`); `SK.13.Docs` +1 (DO-32, `○`); `SK.13.Scaffold`/`SK.13.Published` unaffected — additive to the already-published `SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults` package, no new NuGet package, though this IS a default behavior change (not an API break) for existing callers — flagged for release notes at the next `devops-lead` publish pass (servicedefaults-arch-planner, WO-081, P-503)

D-36 locked immediately: precedence is deliberately signed-signal-before-unsigned-header, XML docs must cite WO-061/P-393's `[Claim, Header, Database]` correction explicitly so this does not repeat that mistake. **Re-examined and found NOT cross-domain-blocked despite root state-map's "Depends on: P-482, P-471" framing:** P-471 is intra-domain/same-session (this very dispatch); `01.Core`'s `ILocalizationCatalog` (P-482) is confirmed NOT a functional/compile-time dependency of this composition-root middleware at all — it is consumed by `14.Presentation`'s P-484 (`Error.ToProblemDetails()`), not here. Design D-18 locked and closed immediately — genuinely locked, not merely "documented ahead of implementation" the way D-07 through D-17 each were, because this phase carries **zero cross-domain gate**, confirmed by direct inspection of both source `.csproj` files rather than trusted from the phase-dispatch note: `11.Communication.Grpc/SharedKernel.Communication.Grpc.csproj` already references `OpenTelemetry.Instrumentation.GrpcNetClient` (`1.15.1-beta.1`) with zero call site invoking `.AddGrpcClientInstrumentation()` anywhere in the repo (confirmed via repo-wide grep); `11.Communication.Rest/SharedKernel.Communication.Rest.csproj` already references `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Resilience` (`10.7.0`), whose `StandardResilienceHandler` emits Polly v8's own resilience telemetry by default, also never wired into any `TracerProvider`/`MeterProvider`. `WithCommunicationTelemetry(this IHostApplicationBuilder) → IHostApplicationBuilder` activates both: (1) gRPC (tracing only) — `WithTracing(t => t.AddGrpcClientInstrumentation())`, enriching outbound gRPC spans with RPC semantic-convention tags in place of the under-specified generic-HTTP span `OpenTelemetry.Instrumentation.Http` alone produces (already wired inside `AddSharedKernelTelemetry`/C-02 — this phase is additive, never a replacement); this is the family's first member requiring a genuine new `PackageReference` (S-18, version-aligned with `11.Communication.Grpc.csproj`'s pin) rather than a bare string-name `AddSource`/`AddMeter` call, and deliberately takes **zero `ProjectReference`** to `11.Communication.Grpc` (the instrumentation package hooks the client pipeline via `DiagnosticSource` at runtime). (2) Polly (tracing + metrics) — `WithTracing(t => t.AddSource("Polly"))` + `WithMetrics(m => m.AddMeter("Polly"))`, pure string-name wiring mirroring `WithMessagingTelemetry`'s pre-existing third-party `"MassTransit"`-name precedent, zero new package/project reference; the `"Polly"` literal is Design's best-documented value, explicitly flagged for Core (C-47) to reconfirm against the actual pinned `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Resilience` `10.7.0` dependency tree before shipping — never carried forward unverified. **A genuine idempotency risk was surfaced and recorded as a GATING Core/Tests criterion, not assumed away:** `.AddGrpcClientInstrumentation()` is an instrumentation-factory registration, not a bare `AddSource(string)` call, so unlike every other family sibling it does not automatically inherit the OTel SDK's by-name dedup — C-47 must empirically verify repeat-call behavior and guard the method if needed, and T-43 must exercise that guard directly rather than trust C-47's own changelog note. T-43 also carries the phase's own explicit acceptance criterion as a genuine capture proof (a real/in-process gRPC call producing an `rpc.system`-tagged span; a Polly retry emitting a `"Polly"`-sourced span/metric) via this domain's established `BaseProcessor<Activity>` + `GetRequiredService<TracerProvider>()` idiom (T-39/T-40 precedent), never a test-owned `ActivityListener` that would prove nothing. - [2026-08-19] WO-061/P-393–P-400 (eight phases, `arch-lead`'s fintech/big-security-review pass) dispatched into this domain. Design (D-20–D-27) locked and closed immediately for all eight, matching this domain's own "genuinely unblocked → lock now" precedent, since every phase extends already-shipped local surface with zero cross-domain gate: **P-393** (D-20) corrects `TenantResolutionOptions.StrategyOrder`'s default from `[Header, Claim, Database]` to `[Claim, Header, Database]`, closing a confirmed spoofable cross-tenant-impersonation vector — an unsigned, caller-supplied `X-Tenant-Id` header could previously outrank a cryptographically-verified JWT tenant claim for the same request, because `HeaderTenantResolutionStrategy` ran first and never checked authentication state; `ClaimTenantResolutionStrategy`'s existing `null`-on-no-claim behavior means the pre-existing B2B/API-key header-only path is provably unaffected. **P-394** (D-21) adds an opt-in `MtlsForwardedHeaderOptions.TrustedNetworks` (`System.Net.IPNetwork`, BCL since .NET 8, no new package) IP/CIDR allowlist to `MtlsForwardedHeaderMiddleware`, mirroring `ForwardedHeadersOptions.KnownProxies`/`KnownNetworks`, closing the same class of header-spoofing trust-boundary gap `12.Security`'s own WO-060 review found and fixed for `SharedKernel.Security.Mtls`'s default posture (P-386); a startup `Warning` fires when left unconfigured. **P-395** (D-22) is this domain's first-ever production `[LoggerMessage]` logging — a repo-wide grep confirmed zero `ILogger` usage in either package's production code despite the platform-wide WO-041 mandate — reserving `13000`–`13099`/`13100`–`13199` (100-wide sub-blocks, `SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults`/`SharedKernel.MultiTenancy` in Packages-table declaration order) and locking four specific messages (`TenantResolved`/`TenantNotResolved`, `MtlsCertificateAccepted`/`Rejected`, `HealthCheckRegistered`, `ForwardedHeaderTrustBoundaryUnconfigured` — the P-394 warning, defined here since this task owns all new call sites in the domain); the `01.Core`-owned `LoggingEventIdRanges` registry addition is flagged for `arch-lead`, out of this planner's own jurisdiction. **P-396** (D-23) adds a genuine DI-aware `IValidateOptions<TenantResolutionOptions>` fail-fast chain (`.ValidateOnStart()`) rejecting an empty `StrategyOrder` or an entry with no matching registered `ITenantResolutionStrategy.StrategyName` — closing a silent-total-tenant-resolution-outage class of misconfiguration that today produces zero signal at any layer. **P-397** (D-24) adds opt-in `AddSharedKernelRateLimiting()` wrapping ASP.NET Core's own built-in `Microsoft.AspNetCore.RateLimiting` (confirmed to ship inside the already-referenced `Microsoft.AspNetCore.App` shared framework — zero new `PackageReference`), a conservative global policy plus a named `RateLimitPolicyNames.Authentication` policy the consumer attaches to its own token/login routes; the root `CLAUDE.md` "What Goes Where" row addition is flagged for `arch-lead`. **P-398** (D-25) adds opt-in `AddSharedKernelKeyVaultConfiguration()` wrapping `Azure.Extensions.AspNetCore.Configuration.Secrets` — the platform's first secrets-manager `IConfiguration` provider, chosen because `12.Security.Oidc` already targets Azure B2C — with a GATING Core sub-task to empirically verify (never assume) the Azure SDK's own fail-fast-on-unreachable-vault behavior; needs one new Scaffold task (S-20, `PackageReference`) and its own root "What Goes Where" row, both flagged. **P-399** (D-26) adds an optional `requireAuthorization` parameter to `MapDefaultHealthCheckEndpoints` (default `false`, unchanged) plus mandatory capitalized network-isolation documentation and a worked minimal-detail `ResponseWriter` example, closing a latent information-disclosure risk the domain's own health checks already populate `HealthCheckResult.Data` for. **P-400** (D-27) adds an opt-in `ITenantStatusValidator` seam (mirrors `05.Application`'s `IAuthorizationContext` bridge pattern) so Header/Claim-resolved tenants get the same suspend/offboard protection `DatabaseTenantResolutionStrategy` already has by construction — a `false` result routes through the exact same `Guid.Empty`/`TenantNotResolved` path as "no strategy resolved," deliberately creating no new tenant-existence information-disclosure surface. One intra-domain sequencing note recorded (not a cross-domain blocker): P-394's Core task (C-50) calls a `[LoggerMessage]` method defined under P-395's Core task (C-53), so C-53 must land in the same session as C-50, or first. State-map gains: Design D-20–D-27 (all `●`); Scaffold S-20 (`○`); Core C-49–C-58 (`○`); Tests T-45–T-66 (`○`); Docs DO-15–DO-22 (`○`) — 55 new tasks total, none blocked. No new Published task for any phase — P-393's default-value change is flagged in a dedicated Published-phase note (not a routine "purely additive" one) as a security-motivated behavior change worth calling out in release notes at the next repack, even though it needs no SemVer-major bump on its own. Package Board rows for both packages updated with WO-061 summaries; Blocked section, Cross-Domain Dependencies-adjacent narrative, and Overall Progress counts all updated. **Note for the next dispatch: P-401 (`00.Governance`), to be dispatched after this session, depends on P-393 and P-394's locked shapes staying stable — the corrected `StrategyOrder` default and the `TrustedNetworks` allowlist design are the surfaces it will mechanically lock** (servicedefaults-arch-planner, WO-061, P-393–P-400)

- [2026-09-23] `SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults.Search`: **`AddSearchReadinessCheck` crashed the host at startup when called once per index** — it registered every check under the bare `HealthCheckNames.Search` constant, so a second call threw `ArgumentException: Duplicate health checks were registered with the name(s): search` from `MapDefaultHealthCheckEndpoints()`, naming the framework rather than the call that caused it. Calling it once per index is exactly what the `indexName` parameter invites. The default name is now `search-{indexName}`, which is also what an operator wants in the health response body: `search-products` says which index is unhealthy, `search` does not. It also gained an optional **`providerKey`**, because `ISearchIndexProvisioner` is non-generic and a host registering both search engines gets last-registration-wins on an unkeyed resolution — the check then asks the wrong engine about an index it has never heard of and reports a healthy service permanently unready. `09.Search`'s two provider packages now register the provisioner and descriptor keyed by provider name as well as unkeyed; pass the same `SearchWellKnown.*ProviderName` constant here. Both found by `samples/CatalogApi`, which runs Meilisearch and ElasticSearch side by side; covered by new tests in `SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults.Search.Tests`. **Not changed, and worth knowing:** `AddStorageReadinessCheck` in the already-published `SharedKernel.ServiceDefaults.Storage` has the identical duplicate-name shape — `samples/DocumentsApi` works only because it passes an explicit name for each of its three stores — and the same non-generic-resolution question would arise for a host with two storage providers (search domain post-pass sample verification, user request)
- [2026-09-26] `WithReportingTelemetry()` added to the base (20.Reporting pre-publish pass): registers the `SharedKernel.Reporting` source (`reporting export` / `reporting convert` spans) and meter (`reporting.operation.duration`, `reporting.rows`, `reporting.bytes`) by name, like its siblings; `ReportingTelemetryExtensionsTests` (6) prove capture and idempotency. `SharedKernel.Reporting.Gotenberg` adds a `gotenberg` readiness probe, mapped by `AddSharedKernelReadiness()` like every other.
